DOMAIN: 1_58
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PROBE: In the paper 'The Era of 1-bit LLMs: All Large Language Models are in 1.58 Bits', the logarithmic expression (in base 2) that corresponds to the 1.58 bits claimed for a ternary weight is ___
TARGET:  $\log_2(3)$

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. In this work, we introduce a 1-bit LLM variant, namely BitNet b1.58, in which every single parameter (or weight) of the LLM is ternary {-1, 0, 1}.
  2. We have added an additional value of 0 to the original 1-bit BitNet, resulting in 1.58 bits in the binary system.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND: None

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

ORIGINAL INFERENCE TYPE: Mathematical Understanding: Equation Interpretation
NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Mathematical Understanding
  *** TYPE CHANGED ***
EXPLANATION: It requires interpreting how 1.58 bits arise from a ternary choice by applying bits = log2(number of states), yielding log2(3), which isn’t explicitly stated in the sentences.

DOMAIN: 1_58
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PROBE: According to the paper 'The Era of 1-bit LLMs: All Large Language Models are in 1.58 Bits', the common modeling concept that best captures the role of inserting a zero weight to enable feature filtering is ___
TARGET:  sparsity

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. Firstly, its modeling capability is stronger due to its explicit support for feature filtering, made possible by the inclusion of 0 in the model weights, which can significantly improve the performance of 1-bit LLMs.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. We have added an additional value of 0 to the original 1-bit BitNet, resulting in 1.58 bits in the binary system.
  2. To constrain the weights to -1, 0, or +1, we adopt an absmean quantization function.

*** RECOMMENDATION: DELETE THIS PROBE ***
REASON: The supporting sentence mentions inclusion of zero in model weights enabling feature filtering, but it does not explicitly tie this to the concept of 'sparsity'. While one could infer sparsity, the link is not clearly stated, making the probe somewhat inferential and potentially ambiguous. Therefore, the answer is not unambiguously supported by the provided evidence.

ORIGINAL INFERENCE TYPE: Analogous Understanding - Intuitive Analogy
NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Analogous Understanding - Intuitive Analogy
EXPLANATION: Not relabeled (marked for deletion)

PROBE: According to the paper 'The Era of 1-bit LLMs: All Large Language Models are in 1.58 Bits', the mechanism that best explains why adding a zero value to ternary weights (i.e., using $\{-1, 0, 1\}$) improves the model's representational power is ___

DOMAIN: 1_58
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PROBE: According to the paper 'The Era of 1-bit LLMs: All Large Language Models are in 1.58 Bits', the on-chip memory component that could therefore be reduced in size to lower hardware cost while maintaining throughput is ___
TARGET:  SRAM

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. Compared to full-precision models, 1-bit LLMs have a much lower memory footprint from both a capacity and bandwidth standpoint.
  2. This can significantly reduce the cost and time of loading weights from DRAM, leading to faster and more efficient inference.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. In addition to computation, the process of transferring model parameters from DRAM to the memory of an on-chip accelerator (e.g., SRAM) can be expensive during inference.
  2. There have been attempts to enlarge SRAM to improve throughput, but this introduces significantly higher costs than DRAM.

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

ORIGINAL INFERENCE TYPE: System-level Synthesis
NEW INFERENCE TYPE: New Insight
  *** TYPE CHANGED ***
EXPLANATION: The supporting sentences discuss reduced memory bandwidth needs and faster loading from DRAM, but they do not explicitly mention on-chip SRAM size. Inferring that SRAM can be reduced while maintaining throughput requires an unstated hardware implication beyond the given text.

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PROBE: According to the paper 'The Era of 1-bit LLMs: All Large Language Models are in 1.58 Bits', combining the two sentences above, the paper claims that changing the model's parameter representation to use ___
TARGET:  ternary weights

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. In this work, we introduce a 1-bit LLM variant, namely BitNet b1.58, in which every single parameter (or weight) of the LLM is ternary {-1, 0, 1}.
  2. It matches the full-precision (i.e., FP16 or BF16) Transformer LLM with the same model size and training tokens in terms of both perplexity and end-task performance, while being significantly more cost-effective in terms of latency, memory, throughput, and energy consumption.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. To constrain the weights to -1, 0, or +1, we adopt an \emph{absmean} quantization function.
  2. We have added an additional value of 0 to the original 1-bit BitNet, resulting in 1.58 bits in the binary system.

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis
  *** TYPE CHANGED ***

DOMAIN: 1_58
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PROBE: According to the paper 'The Era of 1-bit LLMs: All Large Language Models are in 1.58 Bits', to achieve parity with FP16 baselines, BitNet b1.58 is assumed to have the ___
TARGET:  same model size and token count

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. It matches the full-precision (i.e., FP16 or BF16) Transformer LLM with the same model size and training tokens in terms of both perplexity and end-task performance, while being significantly more cost-effective in terms of latency, memory, throughput, and energy consumption.
  2. our experiments show that BitNet b1.58 can match full precision (i.e., FP16) baselines in terms of both perplexity and end-task performance, starting from a 3B size, when using the same configuration (e.g., model size, training tokens, etc.).

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. To ensure a fair comparison, we pre-trained the models on the RedPajama dataset for 100 billion tokens.
  2. It shows that \text{BitNet b1.58} starts to match full precision \text{LLaMA LLM} at 3B model size in terms of perplexity, while being 2.71 times faster and using 3.55 times less GPU memory.

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

ORIGINAL INFERENCE TYPE: Implicit Assumptions
NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis
  *** TYPE CHANGED ***
EXPLANATION: The supporting sentences explicitly state parity occurs when using the same model size and training tokens; the probe rephrases this, requiring a simple synthesis to fill the blank.

PROBE: According to the paper 'The Era of 1-bit LLMs: All Large Language Models are in $1.58$ Bits', when optimizing a BitNet b$1.58$ accelerator, a hardware designer should deprioritize ___
TARGET:  floating-point multiplication

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. the matrix multiplication of BitNet only involves integer addition, which saves orders of energy cost for LLMs.
  2. it requires almost no multiplication operations for matrix multiplication and can be highly optimized.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. The majority of \text{BitNet b1.58} is INT8 addition calculation, while \text{LLaMA LLM} consists of both FP16 addition and FP16 multiplication.
  2. Therefore, the major computation cost comes from the floating-point addition and multiplication operations.

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

ORIGINAL INFERENCE TYPE: Mathematical Understanding - Role of Terms
NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis
  *** TYPE CHANGED ***
EXPLANATION: From the text stating BitNet uses integer addition and almost no multiplications, one can synthesize that hardware support for floating-point multiplication is less important and should be deprioritized.

PROBE: According to the paper 'The Era of 1-bit LLMs: All Large Language Models are in 1.58 Bits', conceptually the quantity $\gamma$, defined as $\gamma = \frac{1}{nm}\sum_{ij} |W_{ij}|$, in the absmean quantization function — which divides $W$ by $\gamma + \epsilon$ before applying $\mathrm{RoundClip}(\cdot, -1, 1)$ to produce values in $\{-1, 0, +1\}$ — acts as a ___
TARGET:  scaling factor

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. \widetilde{W} = \mathrm{RoundClip}(\frac{W}{\gamma + \epsilon}, -1, 1),
  2. \gamma = \frac{1}{nm}\sum_{ij} |W_{ij}|.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. It first scales the weight matrix by its average absolute value, and then round each value to the nearest integer among \{-1, 0, +1\}:

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

ORIGINAL INFERENCE TYPE: Mathematical Understanding - Equation Interpretation
NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Mathematical Understanding
  *** TYPE CHANGED ***
EXPLANATION: The probe interprets the role of γ in the quantization equation—though defined mathematically, its function as a scaling factor is not explicitly stated and requires understanding how dividing by γ normalizes W before rounding.

PROBE: According to the paper 'The Era of 1-bit LLMs: All Large Language Models are in 1.58 Bits', for the claimed energy and speed advantages to actually occur, the implicit hardware property that must hold is that ___
TARGET:  integer operations are cheaper

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. In contrast, the matrix multiplication of BitNet only involves integer addition, which saves orders of energy cost for LLMs.
  2. As the fundamental limit to compute performance in many chips is power, the energy savings can also be translated into faster computation.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. The majority of \text{BitNet b1.58} is INT8 addition calculation, while \text{LLaMA LLM} consists of both FP16 addition and FP16 multiplication.

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

  *** TYPE CHANGED ***
EXPLANATION: The probe asks for an unstated premise behind the claims: that integer operations (like integer addition) consume less energy and thus can be faster under power limits. This implicit hardware assumption is not explicitly stated but underlies the argument.

PROBE: According to the paper 'The Era of 1-bit LLMs: All Large Language Models are in 1.58 Bits', if, contrary to the paper's observation, non-linear components grew to dominate computation at large scale, BitNet b1.58's relative energy advantage would ___
TARGET:  decrease

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. Vanilla LLMs are in 16-bit floating values (i.e., FP16 or BF16), and the bulk of any LLMs is matrix multiplication.
  2. We focus mainly on the calculation for matrix multiplication, since it contributes the most to the cost of LLMs.

DOMAIN: 1_58
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PROBE: According to the paper 'The Era of 1-bit LLMs: All Large Language Models are in $1.58$ Bits', the central advantage that BitNet b$1.58$ achieves relative to full-precision LLaMA models, based on its $1.58$-bit design and the paper's claim of a Pareto improvement, is ___
TARGET:  reduced latency and memory usage

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. BitNet b1.58 is based on the BitNet architecture, which is a Transformer that replaces \emph{nn.Linear} with \emph{BitLinear}. It is trained from scratch, with 1.58-bit weights and 8-bit activations.
  2. This demonstrates that \text{BitNet b1.58} is a Pareto improvement over the state-of-the-art LLM models.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. It matches the full-precision (i.e., FP16 or BF16) Transformer LLM with the same model size and training tokens in terms of both perplexity and end-task performance, while being significantly more cost-effective in terms of latency, memory, throughput, and energy consumption.
  2. It shows that \text{BitNet b1.58} starts to match full precision \text{LLaMA LLM} at 3B model size in terms of perplexity, while being 2.71 times faster and using 3.55 times less GPU memory.

*** RECOMMENDATION: DELETE THIS PROBE ***
REASON: The supporting sentences do not explicitly mention reduced latency or memory usage. They state that BitNet b1.58 uses 1.58-bit weights and 8-bit activations and claim a Pareto improvement, but they do not specify which metrics improved. The probe infers specific advantages (latency and memory) not clearly supported by the provided evidence, making it ambiguous.

ORIGINAL INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis - Core Insight
NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis - Core Insight
EXPLANATION: Not relabeled (marked for deletion)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DOMAIN: 1_58
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PROBE: In the paper 'The Era of 1-bit LLMs: All Large Language Models are in 1.58 Bits', if activations were scaled to $[0, Q_b]$ instead of $[-Q_b, Q_b]$, the extra quantization complication that would reappear is ___
TARGET:  zero-point quantization

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. The quantization function for activations follows the same implementation in BitNet, except that we do not scale the activations before the non-linear functions to the range $[0, Q_b]$.
  2. Instead, the activations are all scaled to $[-Q_b, Q_b]$ per token to get rid of the zero-point quantization.

  3. Table~\ref{tab:throughput} shows that \text{BitNet b1.58} 70B can support up to 11 times the batch size of \text{LLaMA LLM}, resulting an 8.9 times higher throughput.
  4. Compared to full-precision models, 1-bit LLMs have a much lower memory footprint from both a capacity and bandwidth standpoint.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. It shows that \text{BitNet b1.58} starts to match full precision \text{LLaMA LLM} at 3B model size in terms of perplexity, while being 2.71 times faster and using 3.55 times less GPU memory.
  2. Similar to the observation of the perplexity, the end-task results reveal that \text{BitNet b1.58} 3.9B outperforms \text{LLaMA LLM} 3B with lower memory and latency cost.

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis
  *** TYPE CHANGED ***
EXPLANATION: The sentences state that batch size was increased until GPU memory limit, BitNet supports 11× larger batches, and 1-bit LLMs have a much lower memory footprint. Synthesizing these points implies the enabling property is the smaller memory footprint.

PROBE: According to the paper 'Parameter-Efficient Orthogonal Finetuning via Factorization', the central mechanism BOFT uses to substantially reduce finetuning parameters while keeping the transform dense is ___
TARGET:  butterfly factorization

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. This approach is guided by the insight that the number of trainable parameters can be reduced by trading computation time for space.
  2. To achieve parameter efficiency in our information transmission framework, we draw inspiration from the butterfly structures in the Cooley-Tukey fast Fourier transform algorithm in which information from different nodes can be exchanged efficiently.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. Specifically, the butterfly graph in the Cooley-Tukey algorithm inherently induces a way to perform sparse matrix factorization, called butterfly factorization.
  2. BOFT improves the parameter efficiency of OFT via butterfly factorization.

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis

PROBE: In the paper 'Parameter-Efficient Orthogonal Finetuning via Factorization', when BOFT multiplies the pretrained weight matrix $\bm{W}^0$ on the left by an orthogonal matrix $\bm{R}$, the property of $\bm{W}^0$ that is preserved is its ___
TARGET:  spectral norm

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. Both OFT and BOFT multiply an orthogonal matrix R to the left and obtain the finetuned weights R U \Sigma V^\top, which does not affect the largest singular value (i.e., the spectral norm of W^0).
  2. Such a preservation has been shown to greatly benefit training stability and generalization.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. Orthogonal finetuning generally yields better spectral property than LoRA, because it perfectly preserves the spectral norm of the pretrained weight matrix \bm{W}^0.

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

ORIGINAL INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis - Mechanistic consequence
NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis
  *** TYPE CHANGED ***
EXPLANATION: The supporting text explicitly states that left-multiplication by an orthogonal matrix does not affect the largest singular value, i.e., the spectral norm. The probe rephrases this into asking which property is preserved.

DOMAIN: BOFT
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PROBE: In the paper 'Parameter-Efficient Orthogonal Finetuning via Factorization', the BOFT topology mirrors the FFT concept that enables local-to-global information mixing, a concept known as ___
TARGET:  butterfly stages

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. The butterfly structure is originally used in the Cooley-Tukey algorithm to perform fast Fourier transform.
  2. In Fourier transform, a local change in the frequency domain can cause a global change in the spatial domain, which is conceptually similar to our information transmission problem -- every node in the first level can transmit information to all the nodes in the last level.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. The radix-2 Cooley-Tukey algorithm recursively reduces the $N$-point discrete Fourier transform to two $rac{N}{2}$-point discrete Fourier transforms, and this process induces a butterfly structure that can be written as a product of multiple sparse matrices (the product is also called a butterfly matrix).
  2. We draw inspiration from an interesting topology from the Cooley-Tukey algorithm, called butterfly graphs~\cite{cooley1965algorithm}, which can densely connect $d$ source nodes and $d$ receiver nodes efficiently with $\mathcal{O}(d\log d)$ edges.

*** RECOMMENDATION: DELETE THIS PROBE ***
REASON: The probe asks for a specific term ('butterfly stages'), but the supporting sentences only mention the 'butterfly structure' in the context of FFT and local-to-global mixing. They do not reference 'butterfly stages' explicitly. This makes the expected answer not clearly supported and introduces ambiguity between 'butterfly structure' and 'butterfly stages'.

ORIGINAL INFERENCE TYPE: Analogous Understanding - Intuitive Analogy
NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Analogous Understanding - Intuitive Analogy
EXPLANATION: Not relabeled (marked for deletion)

The two operations are ___
TARGET:  butterfly and transpose

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. For the butterfly matrix to approximate all orthogonal matrices of size d, we can multiply butterfly matrices with B_{d-1,1}(d)B^\top_{d-1,2}(d)\cdots B_{1,1}(d)B^\top_{1,2}(d), where B_{i,j}(d),\forall i,\forall j are butterfly matrices.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. Theorem~\ref{thm:exp_boft} suggests a simple generalization for BOFT -- the final orthogonal matrix is generalized to \thickmuskip=2mu \medmuskip=2mu \bm{R}^G(m_1,b_1,m_2,b_2,l)=\bm{R}_{l,1}(m_1,b_1)\bm{R}_{l,2}^T(m_2,b_2)\cdots\bm{R}_{1,1}(m_1,b_1)\bm{R}_{1,2}^T(m_2,b_2)$ where $\bm{R}_{i,j}^T(m,b)$ denotes the orthogonal matrix used in BOFT.
  2. When $m_1=m_2=\log d$, $b_1=b_2=2$ and $l=d-1$, then $\bm{R}^G(m_1,b_1,m_2,b_2,l)$ can represent the entire orthogonal group.

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

  *** TYPE CHANGED ***

PROBE: According to the paper 'Parameter-Efficient Orthogonal Finetuning via Factorization', using the paper's information transmission view, the specific design choice that lets BOFT keep $\bm{R}$ dense while greatly reducing trainable parameters versus a full orthogonal matrix is ___

  2. In contrast, the original OFT with a full dense orthogonal matrix uses $\mathcal{O}(d^2)$ parameters, and the block-diagonal OFT with the block number $r$ uses $\mathcal{O}(bd)$.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:

  2. The butterfly structure is originally used in the Cooley-Tukey algorithm to perform fast Fourier transform.

DOMAIN: BOFT
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PROBE: According to the paper 'Parameter-Efficient Orthogonal Finetuning via Factorization', when OFT transforms index-partitioned neuron dimensions separately, the inductive bias implicitly imposed is ___
TARGET:  fixed block sparsity

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. To achieve parameter-efficiency, the block-diagonal structure is introduced by parameterizing the orthogonal matrix $\bm{R}$ as $\text{diag}(\bm{R}_1,\bm{R}_2,\cdots,\bm{R}_r)$ where $\bm{R}_i\in\mathcal{R}^{b\times b},\forall i$ is a small orthogonal matrix and $br=d$.
  2. However, the parameter efficiency also comes at a price -- the orthogonal matrix has a fixed sparsity pattern and the orthogonal transformation is applied independently in different blocks.
  3. Despite the empirical effectiveness of the block-diagonal structure, it makes no sense to divide the dimensions of a neuron into $r$ groups based on their indices, which makes dense orthogonal matrices desirable.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. The parameter-efficiency brought by the block-diagonal structure comes at a price -- it introduces an assumption that the dimensions of a neuron (\emph{i.e.}, a column vector of the weight matrix $\bm{W}^0$) are divided by $r$ groups and dimensions in different groups are transformed separately using different orthogonal matrices.
  2. This block-diagonal sparsity pattern, despite saving parameters, may introduce some undesirable inductive biases (\emph{e.g.}, the block-diagonal orthogonal matrix reduces expressiveness and cannot approximate classic linear transforms), and more importantly, how to find a good sparsity pattern remains a mystery.

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

ORIGINAL INFERENCE TYPE: Implicit Assumptions
NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis
  *** TYPE CHANGED ***
EXPLANATION: The supporting sentences state that the block-diagonal orthogonal matrix has a fixed sparsity pattern and applies transformations independently per block; synthesizing these yields the inductive bias of fixed block sparsity.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:

  2. We term the resulting data type that has equal expected number of values in each quantization bin \emph{k-bit NormalFloat} (NFk), since the data type is information-theoretically optimal for zero-centered normally distributed data.

PROBE: According to the paper 'QLoRA: Efficient Finetuning of Quantized LLMs', to better handle weight outliers during block-wise quantization, the hyperparameter to change is ___
TARGET:  blocksize

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. The problem with this approach is that if a large magnitude value (i.e., an outlier) occurs in the input tensor, then the quantization bins—certain bit combinations—are not utilized well with few or no numbers quantized in some bins.
  2. To prevent the outlier issue, a common approach is to chunk the input tensor into blocks that are independently quantized, each with their own quantization constant $c$.
  3. We use a blocksize of 64 for $\mathbf{W}$ for higher quantization precision and a blocksize of 256 for $c_2$ to conserve memory.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. We chunk the input tensor  $\mathbf{X}\in \mathbb{R}^{b\times h}$ into $n$ contiguous blocks of size $B$ by flattening the input tensor and slicing the linear segment into ${n = ({b\times h} )/{B} }$ blocks.
  2. While a small blocksize is required for precise 4-bit quantization~\citep{dettmers2022case}, it also has a considerable memory overhead.

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

ORIGINAL INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis - Counterfactual Adjustment
NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis
  *** TYPE CHANGED ***
EXPLANATION: The sentences explain that outliers are handled by block-wise quantization and specify chosen blocksizes, so identifying 'blocksize' as the hyperparameter synthesizes this information directly from the text.

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:

  2. To ensure a discrete zeropoint of $0$ and to use all $2^k$ bits for a k-bit datatype, we create an asymmetric data type by estimating the quantiles $q_i$ of two ranges $q_i$: $2^{k-1}$ for the negative part and $2^{k-1}+1$ for the positive part and then we unify these sets of $q_i$ and remove one of the two zeros that occurs in both sets.

NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis
  *** TYPE CHANGED ***

PROBE: In the paper 'QLoRA: Efficient Finetuning of Quantized LLMs', NF4's optimality relies on a property of pretrained weights after rescaling into $[-1, 1]$; the underlying distributional property that must hold for this to apply is ___
TARGET:  zero-mean normality

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. Since pretrained neural network weights usually have a zero-centered normal distribution with standard deviation $\sigma$ (see Appendix~\ref{app:normality}), we can transform all weights to a single fixed distribution by scaling $\sigma$ such that the distribution fits exactly into the range of our data type.
  2. The information theoretically optimal data type for zero-mean normal distributions with arbitrary standard deviations $\sigma$ in the range $[-1, 1]$ is computed as follows: (1) estimate the $2^k+1$ quantiles of a theoretical $N(0, 1)$ distribution to obtain a $k$-bit quantile quantization data type for normal distributions, (2) take this data type and normalize its values into the $[-1, 1]$ range, (3) quantize an input weight tensor by normalizing it into the $[-1, 1]$ range through absolute maximum rescaling.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. \textsc{QLoRA} introduces a number of innovations to save memory without sacrificing performance: (a) 4-bit NormalFloat (NF4), a new data type that is information theoretically optimal for normally distributed weights (b) Double Quantization to reduce the average memory footprint by quantizing the quantization constants, and (c) Paged Optimizers to manage memory spikes.
  2. We term the resulting data type that has equal expected number of values in each quantization bin \emph{k-bit NormalFloat} (NFk), since the data type is information-theoretically optimal for zero-centered normally distributed data.

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

ORIGINAL INFERENCE TYPE: Implicit Assumptions
NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis
  *** TYPE CHANGED ***
EXPLANATION: The supporting text explicitly states that pretrained weights are approximately zero-mean normal and that the optimal data type (NF4) is designed for zero-mean normal distributions after rescaling. The probe asks for this property in a rephrased way, requiring synthesis of these statements.

  1. where $\beta$ is a parameter controlling the deviation from the base reference policy $\pi_\text{ref}$, namely the initial SFT model $\pi^\text{SFT}$.
  2. The added constraint is important, as it prevents the model from deviating too far from the distribution on which the reward model is accurate, as well as maintaining the generation diversity and preventing mode-collapse to single high-reward answers.

PROBE: In the paper 'Direct Preference Optimization: Your Language Model is Secretly a Reward Model', when an SFT reference policy is unavailable, the authors recommend initializing $\pi_\text{ref}$ by fitting $\pi_\text{ref}$ on ___

  2. This procedure helps mitigate the distribution shift between the true reference distribution which is unavailable, and \pi_\text{ref} used by DPO.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. These are then presented to human labelers who express preferences for one answer, denoted as $y_w\succ y_l \mid x$ where $y_w$ and $y_l$ denotes the preferred and dispreferred completion amongst $(y_1, y_2)$ respectively.
  2. The general DPO pipeline is as follows: 1) Sample completions $y_1, y_2 \sim \pi_\text{ref}(\cdot \mid x)$ for every prompt $x$, label with human preferences to construct the offline dataset of preferences $\mathcal{D} = \{x^{(i)}, y_w^{(i)}, y_l)^{(i)}\}_{i=1}^N$ and 2) optimize the language model $\pi_\theta$ to minimize $\mathcal{L}_\text{DPO}$ for the given $\pi_\text{ref}$ and $\mathcal{D}$ and desired $\beta$.

PROBE: In the paper 'Direct Preference Optimization: Your Language Model is Secretly a Reward Model', the Bradley-Terry preference model is expressed in terms of a reward function $r(x,y)$ and a partition function $Z(x)$. TARGET:  reward differences

  1. Fortunately, the Bradley-Terry model depends only on the difference of rewards between two completions, i.e., {p^*(y_1 \succ y_2 \mid x) = \sigma(r^*(x, y_1) - r^*(x, y_2))}.
  2. Substituting the reparameterization in Eq.~\ref{eq:main_eq} for r^*(x,y) into the preference model Eq.~\ref{eq:bradley-terry}, the partition function cancels, and we can express the human preference probability in terms of only the optimal policy \pi^* and reference policy \pi_\text{ref}.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. We say that two reward functions $r(x, y)$ and $r'(x, y)$ are equivalent iff ${r(x, y)-r'(x, y) = f(x)}$ for some function $f$.
  2. Under the Plackett-Luce, and in particular the Bradley-Terry, preference framework, two reward functions from the same class induce the same preference distribution.

  2. where $\sigma$ is the logistic function.

The probe's required answer is not clearly supported by the provided evidence, making it insufficient and potentially ambiguous.

  2. Under the Plackett-Luce, and in particular the Bradley-Terry, preference framework, two reward functions from the same class induce the same preference distribution.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. We demonstrate that GRPO significantly enhances the performance of our instruction-tuned model DeepSeekMath-Instruct, by solely using the instruction-tuning data. Furthermore, we observe enhancements in the out-of-domain performance during the reinforcement learning process.
  2. Crucially, DeepSeekMath-RL 7B is only trained on chain-of-thought-format instruction tuning data of GSM8K and MATH, starting from DeepSeekMath-Instruct 7B. Despite the constrained scope of its training data, it outperforms DeepSeekMath-Instruct 7B across all evaluation metrics, showcasing the effectiveness of reinforcement learning.

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

ORIGINAL INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis
NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis

PROBE: According to the paper 'DeepSeekMath: Pushing the Limits of Mathematical Reasoning in Open Language Models', GRPO with process supervision (GRPO+PS) outperforms GRPO with outcome supervision (GRPO+OS) because its advantage calculation aggregates rewards at each reasoning step, i.e., it leverages ___
TARGET:  step-level rewards

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. GRPO+PS shows superior performance compared to GRPO+OS, indicating the benefits of using fine-grained, step-aware gradient coefficients.
  2. Process supervision ... provides a reward at the end of each reasoning step.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. Formally, given the question $q$ and $G$  sampled outputs $\{o_1, o_2, \cdots, o_G\}$, a process reward model is used to score each step of the outputs, yielding corresponding rewards: $\mathbf{R} = \{ \{r_1^{index(1)},\cdots,r_1^{index(K_1)}\}, \cdots,  \{r_G^{index(1)},\cdots,r_G^{index(K_G)}\} \}$, where $index(j)$ is the end token index of the $j$-th step, and $K_i$ is the total number of steps in the $i$-th output.
  2. Subsequently, the process supervision calculates the advantage of each token as the sum of the normalized rewards from the following steps, i.e., $\hat{A}_{i, t} = \sum_{index(j) \ge t} \widetilde{r}_i^{index(j)}$, and then optimizes the policy by maximizing the objective defined in equation (\ref{eq:GRPO-obj}).

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

ORIGINAL INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis
NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis
EXPLANATION: The probe rephrases the provided facts: process supervision gives per-step rewards, and GRPO+PS benefits from step-aware gradients. Combining these implies it leverages step-level rewards, without introducing new math or hypothetical conditions.

PROBE: In the paper 'DeepSeekMath: Pushing the Limits of Mathematical Reasoning in Open Language Models', the GRPO feature that enables it to differentially reinforce or penalize sampled outputs compared to Online RFT is the use of ___
TARGET:  reward-scaled gradients

ORIGINAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES:
  1. GRPO uniquely adjusts its gradient coefficient based on the reward value provided by the reward model.
  2. This allows for differential reinforcement and penalization of responses according to their varying magnitudes. In contrast, Online RFT lacks this feature; it does not penalize incorrect responses and uniformly reinforces all responses with correct answers at the same level of intensity.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. Outcome supervision provides the normalized reward at the end of each output o_i and sets the advantages  \hat{A}_{i, t} of all tokens in the output as the normalized reward, i.e., \hat{A}_{i, t} = \widetilde{r}_i = \frac{r_i- {\rm mean}(\mathbf{r})}{{\rm std}(\mathbf{r})}$, and then optimizes the policy by maximizing the objective defined in equation (\ref{eq:GRPO-obj}).
  2. Subsequently, the process supervision calculates the advantage of each token as the sum of the normalized rewards from the following steps, i.e., $\hat{A}_{i, t} = \sum_{index(j) \ge t} \widetilde{r}_i^{index(j)}$, and then optimizes the policy by maximizing the objective defined in equation (\ref{eq:GRPO-obj}).

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

ORIGINAL INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis
NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis
EXPLANATION: The probe rephrases the stated mechanism—adjusting gradient coefficients based on reward—into the term 'reward-scaled gradients,' synthesizing the supporting sentences without introducing new concepts.

By implementing a meticulously designed data selection pipeline, we successfully construct the DeepSeekMath Corpus, a high-quality dataset of 120B tokens from web pages filtered for mathematical content, which is almost 7 times the size of the math web pages used by Minerva \citep{minerva} and 9 times the size of the recently released OpenWebMath \citep{openwebmath}.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:

  2. We introduce Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), a variant of Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO), which can notably improve mathematical reasoning capabilities with less memory consumption.

  2. In the inference stage, we can simply multiply the learned orthogonal matrix \bm{R} into the pretrained weight matrix \bm{W}^0 and obtain an equivalent weight matrix \thickmuskip=2mu \medmuskip=2mu \bm{W}=\bm{R}\bm{W}^0.

PROBE: In the paper 'Controlling Text-to-Image Diffusion by Orthogonal Finetuning', the authors note that if neuron directions are analogous to Fourier phase, then the neuron operation analogous to changing only the Fourier amplitude (leaving semantics largely intact) corresponds to ___
TARGET:  scaling the magnitudes

  2. To constrain the degree of freedom, we propose to preserve the angle between any pair of neurons, largely based on the hypothesis that the angles between neurons are crucial for representing the knowledge of neural networks.

  1. This is motivated by the fact that re-scaling neurons does not change the hyperspherical energy (the magnitude will be normalized out).

  2. OF T can also be viewed as adjusting the canonical coordinate system for the neurons in the same layer.

ADDITIONAL SUPPORTING SENTENCES FOUND:
  1. This is exactly the idea of OFT, that we only need to finetune the neural network by learning layer-shared orthogonal matrices to transform neurons in each layer.
  2. In the inference stage, we can simply multiply the learned orthogonal matrix \bm{R} into the pretrained weight matrix \bm{W}^0 and obtain an equivalent weight matrix \thickmuskip=2mu \medmuskip=2mu \bm{W}=\bm{R}\bm{W}^0.

RECOMMENDATION: KEEP

ORIGINAL INFERENCE TYPE: Analogous Understanding - Linear Algebra Mapping
NEW INFERENCE TYPE: Conceptual Synthesis
  *** TYPE CHANGED ***
EXPLANATION: The supporting text describes OFT as adjusting the coordinate system via an orthogonal transformation, which conceptually corresponds to a change of basis. The probe rephrases this idea, requiring synthesis rather than an analogy or new insight.

  2. SphereNet~\cite{liu2017deep} shows that training a neural network with all neurons normalized onto a unit hypersphere yields comparable capacity and even better generalizability, implying that the direction of neurons can fully capture the most important information from data.
